Rubric-vs-plan check: no task mandates an assertion-free test; the census guards assert exact name lists by design (判据 §3) — that is the guard shape, not verbatim duplication.

Pre-flight rulings:
- PF-1 Ruling: the master plan's "The worktree has its own `target/`" is false — `/Volumes/TBU4/Workspace/Aleph/.cargo/config.toml` (discovered by parent walk) pins ONE shared `target-dir` for main and every worktree. Only two worktrees exist (main @ `3ddc1f2e7`, ours). Consequence carried into every QA-stage dispatch: build from this worktree immediately before the run, nobody else builds meanwhile (memory: a shared target once ran the other worktree's binary). Plan line corrected in a docs commit. — cost if wrong: none beyond a stale sentence.
- PF-2 Ruling: `P4-cc-compat.md` P4.7 preamble names `unregister_skills_for_plugin`; the true name is `ToolCatalog::unregister_skills(&[String])` (R1.8). Corrected in the same docs commit; P4.7 dispatches carry the true name. — cost if wrong: none (prose only).
- PF-3 Ruling: `ALEPH_ACTIVATION_GATE` is defined by P3.4, but P1.16 and P2.11 (which land earlier) export it. Exporting an env var the binary does not yet read is harmless (a no-op export); the stages' assertions do not depend on it until P3.4. Dispatch P1.16/P2.11 with the export as written (no reorder). — cost if wrong: one stage re-run after P3.4.
- PF-4 Ruling: no TodoWrite tool in this session — this ledger IS the todo list; the "Order" line above is the task list, completion lines below mark progress.
- PF-5 Ruling: P0 touches nothing in the tree (scratchpad + `claude -p`) → it runs in parallel with P1.1; its output table is saved to `p0-capture-table.md` in this workspace and handed to P4.3. — cost if wrong: none (the one-agent rule is about the tree).
- PF-6 Model policy: implementers sonnet for briefs that carry complete code (transcription + tests) and for doc-only tasks; opus for integration/judgment tasks (P1.8, P1.9, P1.10, P1.11, P1.15, P2.2, P2.3, P2.8, P3.3, P3.4, P4.3, P4.4, P4.7a–c, P4.8, P4.10, P5.4–P5.6, P6.3, P6.4, P6.6, P6.7, P6.9, P7.1) and all QA stages; reviewers sonnet for small diffs, opus for the judgment tasks; final review opus.

- Process mechanism (from verify-P1): a foreground Bash that exceeds the 10-min cap is AUTO-backgrounded by the harness; the agent then idles waiting. So the rule is not "never background" but "if a command gets auto-backgrounded, poll it with bounded waits and never re-invoke it". Dispatches name it that way from now on.

- Ruling (speed; user asked 2026-09-23 whether to run two parallel lanes): NO second code lane — `/Volumes/TBU4/Workspace/Aleph/.cargo/config.toml` shares ONE target dir on purpose (this 16 GB machine froze 3× on 2026-06-10 at ~7 GB per alephcore rustc); two lanes either queue on that lock (overlap only non-compile time, and lock waits push cargo runs past the 10-min foreground strand) or, with separate target dirs, re-create the freeze — cost if wrong: some hours of wall time. Adopted instead: (1) read-only pre-flight of the NEXT task runs in parallel with the current task's implementer/reviewer (no cargo, no tree writes); (2) per-seat verification = module lib tests RUN + ONE `cargo clippy -p alephcore --all-targets --features test-helpers` (type-checks lib tests, bins, every integration target) + `--bins` RUN only when `src/bin/` or boot wiring is touched; `--lib --no-run` and `--test '*' --no-run` leave the per-task list and join the six-command set at phase ends — cost if wrong: a link-only error surfaces at the phase end, not the task; (3) same-shape small tasks batch into one dispatch (P5 cuts, P8 docs); (4) rust-analyzer flycheck (pid 79288) runs `cargo check --all-targets` on the same target lock with a different cfg — raised to the user (environment is theirs).

- P3.3 in flight — named risk traced REACHABLE (impl-P3-3, before implementing): unmount/`unmount_all`/`fail_mount` dispose the scope but never touch `activation_watchers`; receivers are answered (serial actor) not dropped; `write_readiness` carries no mount identity; scheduler decides whether mount #1's watcher write lands before/after mount #2's — worst cases: a reloaded plugin reads `loaded` while its new server is unanswered, a rolled-back mount (`fail_mount` after `mcp_server`) reads `loaded` PERMANENTLY. Decision (accepted, Ruling): end the watcher inside the mount's own `mcp_server` disposer (abort + wait until the task's future is dropped) rather than a per-mount token on the row — removes the stale writer instead of adding a check every future writer must remember. Deviation: `watch_server_starts` returns `Option<WatcherStop>` (brief said `()`); `activation_settled` must read a cancelled watcher as normal. Cost if wrong: a disposer that waits on a task could deadlock if the task awaits something the disposer holds — reviewer to check.
- P3.3 concern 1 (impl-P3-3): a moment-3 `Error` flips `is_active()` so registry lookups hide the plugin's skills/agents at once, while the published projections + hook executor (derived only in `after_transition`) keep showing them — two representations (判据 §1). Ruling: spec §2 (`design.md:32`) "every path that can change plugin activation calls [the one derivation]" ⇒ a readiness write that changes activity MUST go through `after_transition`; Pending→Loaded needs none. Done as a SECOND P3.3 commit with its own subject + test (failing server ⇒ Error AND skill absent from the published projection) + mutation (delete the recompute call ⇒ red); deadlock check (no recompute under the registry write lock; the abort-and-wait disposer holds nothing the recompute takes). Cost if wrong: one extra commit.

- review-P4-7c (opus, SECURITY, read-only): CHANGES REQUIRED — Critical 1 / Important 4 / Minor 6. The N1 commit `cf2e6f023` is sound and approvable on its own.
  - **S1 CRITICAL.** Deviation 1's claim is false for every bare skill id. The gate judges the catalog row's `owning_plugin`, but the renderer re-resolves the command by SHORT name across all active plugins. So a bundled skill such as `code-review` next to a same-named plugin command runs the HIDDEN plugin's approved inline commands.
  - **S2.** Inline commands run before `UserPromptSubmit` / `BeforeAgentStart` can deny the turn.
  - **S3.** Relative-script consent hashes `<plugin root>/…` while the command executes in the session cwd, so the approval attests to the wrong file.
  - **S4.** `inline_shell_command` never calls `env_clear()`, so the child inherits the WHOLE daemon environment: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, bot tokens, `EMAIL_PASSWORD`, … With an approved `` !`printenv $1` ``, a guest sender picks the variable name.
  - **S5 (DECIDE).** Channel floors are not consulted. A guest sender (`caller_role = "guest"`) on a channel whose operator denied `bash` still runs approved inline shell, unsandboxed. The precedent is that `/moa` arms only for `role_is_operator`.
  - Minors:
    - S6: CC aliases fall back silently.
    - S7: event-less key.
    - S8: cancel, timeout, orphans.
    - S9: `sensitive` plaintext is exported.
    - S10: the settings-form guard is census-only.
    - S11: the busy lane holds the pre-stamp model pin, the "pill says X, answer from Y" case.

  - **S1.** ONE derivation: render only when the mode names an owner; look up the registration by the EXACT key; require `reg.plugin_id == owning_plugin`. Add a red test with a bundled skill and a same-named plugin command.
  - **S2.** Move the render after `UserPromptSubmit` (and `BeforeAgentStart` if it is on the same path), so a deny hook stops inline shell. Red test.
  - **S3.** Refuse relative script tokens in inline commands: withhold them with a visible placeholder. `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/…` stays content-bound.
  - **S4.** `env_clear()` + a documented allowlist in `inline_shell_command` (e.g. PATH, HOME, USER, LOGNAME, LANG, LC_*, TERM, TMPDIR, SHELL, TZ), then the builder's own vars. The hook twin gets the same question: record it (§16), do not change hooks here.
  - **S5 DECIDED (fail closed, /moa precedent).** Inline commands run only for OPERATOR callers AND only when the channel's tool deny layer does not deny `bash`. Otherwise every inline command is withheld with a visible placeholder and the rest of the body still renders. Add tests for guest and deny-layer.

  - S7, S9, S10: document or fix cheaply.

- impl-P4-18 question: the fs red is cache poisoning, not only a race. DENIED_NORM_CACHE compiles '~/.ssh' once, keyed by the raw string. The branch's World fixture (0ea585590, 24 tests, HomeEnvGuards temp HOME, in gateway::execution_engine) runs first alphabetically, so the process then refuses only <dead tempdir>/.ssh. This explains the rise from 2/3 to 2/2. Ruling: option (C), key the cache by the expanded entry (the key is coarser than its derivation, criterion 19), plus reader guards and a RED test for HOME A then HOME B. This overrides the brief's 'do not redesign the cache'. Cost if wrong: an env read plus a join per denied-entry lookup.
